Beyond rescue duty, this route makes the genuine self-test checks possible (real touch emulation, live media flips, URL blocking, real mouse presses); those are listed with the checklist in `scrub-pipeline.md`.

## The page

| Symptom | Cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| An entrance animation never plays, the element just appears | A later rule won the cascade over the animation's starting state | Prefix start and end states with the container class (`.card .part`, `.card.in .part`) and prove every entrance plays |
| Hovers on the 2nd and 3rd items of a staggered grid respond late even after the entrance finished | The cleanup rule that zeroes the stagger `transition-delay` has lower specificity than the nth-child delay rules it retires (`:nth-child` counts as a class), so it silently never applies | Make the cleanup selector match or beat the delay rules (repeat the nth-child in it) or put `!important` on the `0s` delay, then prove it by hovering the later siblings |
| A scroll-driven style stops responding after its entrance | `animation-fill-mode: forwards` overrides it forever | Entrance animation on the parent, dynamic style on a child |
| A background loop flashes or snaps when it starts | Positive animation delay | Negative delays (like `-1.2s`) so every loop is mid-cycle at first paint |
| Letter tails (g, y, p) are cut off | Masked or clipped text with zero breathing room | Em-based padding with matching negative margins on the mask (see `scrub-pipeline.md`) |
| The page can be dragged or shifted sideways | `overflow-x: hidden` alone, or a decoration poking past the edge | `overflow-x: clip` on BOTH `html` and `body`, `hidden` first as fallback |
| Hovers start snapping after a script runs | JavaScript overwrote `el.style.transition` | Toggle a class that declares the full combined transition instead |
| A mobile element sits off-screen with reduced motion on | Blanket `transform: none !important` wiped its positional transform | Re-apply positional transforms per breakpoint inside the reduced-motion block |
| A marquee shows a gap at the loop point | Track shorter than the widest supported screen | Duplicate items until each track exceeds about 2560px |
| Animations run while the tab is hidden or the section is off-screen | Free-running loops | Scope animation rules to a class an IntersectionObserver toggles; on `visibilitychange` toggle one body class with `body.paused *, body.paused *::before, body.paused *::after { animation-play-state: paused !important }`; rAF loops rest when converged |
| A pause written on a container never actually pauses the animations inside it | `animation-play-state` is not an inherited property, so a value set on a parent (or `inherit` on a nested rule) never reaches nested elements or pseudo-elements | The body-class pattern above; it hits every element and pseudo-element directly |

## Deploy

| Symptom | Cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| Live site shows a directory listing or 404 | The zip contains the project folder, not its contents | Re-zip with `index.html` at the zip's top level |
| Images look soft on the live site but sharp locally | The host resizes and recompresses images server-side | Upload larger and cleaner (about 1920px wide, one high-quality pass) so the host's pass is the only lossy step |
| Link previews show no image or the wrong URL | og tags still carry the placeholder | Patch `og:image` and `og:url` with the live absolute URL at the `<!-- DEPLOY STEP -->` comment, re-zip, re-deploy |
| The live site still shows the old version after a re-deploy | Cache | Hard-refresh, or verify against a string you know changed |
| Raw videos or review files appear on the live site | They were inside the deploy folder when it was zipped | Keep raws and review copies OUTSIDE the deploy folder, always |
| Special characters turn to gibberish after a scripted find-and-replace (arrows and ordinal marks become mojibake) | A shell command read the UTF-8 file with the wrong default encoding and wrote the damage back | Never patch site files with plain shell read and write: use the editor tool for the og patch and any text change, or read and write with explicit UTF-8. Recovery if it already happened: read the damaged file as UTF-8, encode that text to Windows-1252 bytes, decode those bytes as UTF-8, save as UTF-8, verify the characters, redeploy |

## Setup

| Symptom | Cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| Hostinger tools missing after adding the connector, or the sign-in page never opens | A freshly added local connector sometimes loads only on the next app start | Close Claude Code completely, reopen, return to the same chat, and trigger the sign-in again |
| The site looks frozen or broken in the app's side preview pane | The built-in pane struggles with scroll-video pages | Open the localhost link in a real web browser; that is the true preview |
| The Hostinger connector command fails on Windows | `npx` instead of `npx.cmd` | Windows uses `claude mcp add hostinger -- npx.cmd -y hostinger-api-mcp` |
| The Hostinger sign-in seems to go nowhere | The authorization opened in a different browser than the user normally uses | Warn them in advance and have them check their other browsers |
| Higgsfield tools missing right after connecting | A freshly added connector sometimes loads only on the next app start | Close Claude Code completely, reopen, return to the same chat, and verify again |
| The Higgsfield connector shows it needs authentication | The sign-in did not complete during the add | Finish it in the connectors panel: the browser opens to Higgsfield to authorize once, then re-verify with a balance call |
| Higgsfield tools present but calls fail | Connector added but not authorized, or no credits | Re-run the sign-in flow; verify with a balance call, which should return real numbers |
| The user says a setup step is done but the next step fails | "Done" was taken as verification | It never is; re-check the system yourself after every step before advancing |
| ffmpeg or Node.js fails to install on a Mac | Homebrew is not installed, so brew commands cannot run, and macOS security often blocks a downloaded binary instead | Have the user install Homebrew first: the one command from brew.sh, pasted into Terminal, which asks for their Mac password. Then run the installs again and verify |
| Claude skips the setup checklist and jumps straight to brand or design questions | The skill zip was never extracted and fully read (common when the zip was dragged into the chat: the project folder stays empty and the skill's files never entered the conversation) | Extract the zip into the project workspace, read SKILL.md and every reference top to bottom, and restart at Phase 1. The checklist message is always the first thing the user sees |
| Every hosting call hangs during a long session and a browser sign-in tab keeps opening | The hosting connector lost its signed-in state mid-session, so every call silently waits on a fresh sign-in | Complete the sign-in once in the tab that opens. If the popup misbehaves, one full app restart plus one sign-in fixes it cleanly. Files and the live site are never affected |

**The two-hang rule (the subsystem rule, for any tool):** a second consecutive hang on the same tool or subsystem means it is down. Stop retrying, name it out loud, and restart. Never make a third call into a hung subsystem. This is different from a slow render: a job that reports progress is working, however long it takes; a call that hangs with no response at all is the one this rule covers.

---
name: premium-scroller-base44-v2
description: Build a premium multi-chapter scrolling website in a native Base44 frontend. Use when a student wants a cinematic, high-end website for a product, place, service, venue or fictional concept.
---

# Premium Scroller v2 for Base44

> Superseded by `../premium-website-builder/SKILL.md`. The earlier claim that five complete motion prototypes had been tested was overstated. This version is retained for history and is not the current tested reference.

This skill builds a complete premium website, not only a video hero. The page has one visual idea, a sequence of designed chapters, a shared motion language and a real conversion path.

The standard output is three to five media chapters joined by normal vertical scrolling. A chapter can be a scroll-controlled video, a still sequence, a product reveal, a horizontal study, a pinned image composition or a calm editorial section. The agent chooses the grammar that suits the business.

This architecture was first tested as five local prototypes: surf coaching, a restaurant, a landscape designer, a residential architect and a precision product. The test showed that the lower page needs its own designed system. A hero followed by generic cards is a failure even when the scroll animation works.

The skill is designed for a Base44 Superagent with a native frontend. Do not use a backend function that returns interactive HTML. Do not call an editor status a finished site. Verify the published frontend.

## The quality bar

The first screen must be impressive before the video loads. The scroll journey must have a beginning, a change, a peak and a landing. The lower page must feel designed with the same care as the hero.

Premium comes from decisions that remain consistent:

- One visual premise for the whole page
- A clear feeling curve: arrival, orientation, movement, peak, proof, action
- Three to five distinct chapters, not one effect repeated everywhere
- One shared easing and duration system
- Large, confident type with short copy
- Real negative space planned for text
- One signature interaction that belongs to the business
- A calm landing section after the biggest moment
- A useful CTA that works on desktop and phone

Do not add effects just to make the page look expensive. Do not use particles, a custom cursor, an intro loader, spinning objects, glowing card grids, fake counters or constant parallax.

## Input

The student may provide only:

- Business type
- Business name
- Location, if relevant
- Main product or service
- Desired customer action
- Real assets or facts available

If the brief is sparse, invent the creative direction, page grammar and section structure. Do not invent factual business claims, prices, reviews, awards, staff, locations or guarantees. Fictional concepts may use invented names and copy, but real businesses require verified facts.

## Conversation protocol

Work in separate messages. Do not send one giant build request before the concept and media plan exist.

### Message 1: brief and concept directions

Ask for the small brief if it was not supplied. Then return three genuinely different concepts. Each concept must include:

1. Visual premise
2. Scroll grammar
3. Hero action
4. Chapter sequence
5. Signature moment
6. Required media
7. Main CTA
8. Mobile fallback

Do not ask the student to design the page. Recommend one concept based on business fit, visual impact and media feasibility.

### Message 2: design package

Write one complete package for the recommended concept before generating media:

- Audience and real customer language, if the business is real
- One sentence positioning
- Feeling curve
- Chapter storyboard
- Exact section copy
- Palette tokens
- Display and body type roles
- Text position in every media frame
- Interaction map
- Mobile and reduced-motion plan
- Asset list

The video is designed for the website. It is not generated first and forced into a page afterwards.

### Message 3: media plan and cost gate

Choose Kie image-to-video when the student has supplied a Kie key through a secret. Never print the key. If Kie is unavailable, use an approved still fallback and say so.

For every video chapter, provide:

- Start-frame prompt
- Motion prompt
- Negative prompt
- Duration
- Resolution and aspect ratio
- Expected credit use if the interface or API exposes it
- Acceptance checks

For Kie image-to-video, use the documented API shape through Node or curl in the sandbox:

```text
POST https://api.kie.ai/api/v1/jobs/createTask
Authorization: Bearer ${KIE_API_KEY}

{
  "model": "kling-2.6/image-to-video",
  "input": {
    "prompt": "...",
    "negative_prompt": "...",
    "image_urls": ["https://..."],
    "sound": false,
    "duration": "5"
  }
}
```

Use the student's saved secret or connector for `KIE_API_KEY`. Never print it or ask the student to paste it into chat. Poll `GET https://api.kie.ai/api/v1/jobs/recordInfo?taskId=...` until success or failure. The current documented Kling 2.6 route supports five seconds. If the account or model exposes ten seconds, report that setting before spending. If the API rejects the duration or reports insufficient credits, stop without retrying a different generation.

Upload the approved start frame through the available Base44 file tool before calling Kie, or use a public URL supplied by the student. Preserve the original and record the returned task ID, duration, resolution, file size and visible credit deduction.

Use one bounded generation per chapter. Do not generate variations without approval. For a chained sequence, extract the final frame from chapter one and use it as the next chapter start frame. Inspect every chapter before moving to the next one.

### Message 4: build

Build the native Base44 frontend from the approved design package. Keep all authored copy and media URLs explicit. Use shared React components for:

- Header and menu
- Scroll chapter controller
- Media fallback
- Chapter captions when useful
- Editorial section rhythm
- Business interaction
- CTA and footer

Do not use Tailwind unless the app actually loads it. Use scoped CSS or the app's existing build system. Do not add a framework or dependency unless it already exists in the app.

### Message 5: QA

Check the actual published route, not only the editor preview. If the app requires the owner to publish, report that clearly and stop before claiming completion.

## Page grammars

Choose one primary grammar and one supporting grammar. Do not combine more than two.

### Passage

The visitor travels through one physical environment. Use for restaurants, hotels, studios, venues and architecture. The camera crosses meaningful thresholds, then lands in a calm editorial section.

### Assembly

The visitor watches a product or service come together. Use for food, furniture, automotive work, landscaping and craft. Each chapter reveals a meaningful part, process or detail.

### Terrain

The visitor moves through a landscape or route. Use for surf schools, outdoor businesses, resorts, property and adventure services. Use a map, route line or location detail only when it helps the story.

### Transformation

The visitor sees a before state become an after state. Use for cleaning, landscaping, renovations, fitness and design. Keep the change factual and avoid invented outcome claims.

### Product study

The visitor studies one object through controlled light, material details, macro crops and precise type. Use for premium products, technology and fashion.

### Editorial sequence

The page alternates bold text chapters, still images, media and quiet proof sections. Use when the brand needs a strong written voice and the available media is limited.

## Scroll engineering

Desktop and wide preview screens may use pinned sections with 180 to 320 viewport-height units, chosen from the number and length of chapters. Normal browser scrolling must remain in control. Never hijack the wheel.

For a video chapter:

- Wait for metadata
- Clamp target time to duration
- Drive time through requestAnimationFrame
- Prevent overlapping seeks
- Skip negligible seeks
- Stop work when offscreen or the tab is hidden
- Clean up listeners and object URLs
- Use a poster before the media is ready
- Hold the final frame before the next chapter

For phones, reduced motion, data saving or failed video:

- Do not download large videos
- Use a composed poster or still in normal document flow
- Remove the long pinned journey
- Keep the copy and CTA immediately visible
- Position the subject deliberately for narrow crops

## Premium composition rules

- Use a maximum of two type roles: display and body
- Use one small label system for chapter metadata
- Use a palette drawn from the media world
- Use one accent colour sparingly
- Change composition between chapters
- Let one image be large and another be quiet
- Use full-bleed media only when the text has a planned safe area
- Use a local scrim behind text instead of darkening every frame
- Use one strong interaction that teaches or sells something
- Keep copy short enough to read while scrolling
- Never label an animation with empty phrases such as “The reveal” or “The moment”
- Captions must communicate value, orientation or a useful story beat

The minimum complete page is:

1. A visual opening with a clear premise
2. A second visual chapter with a different composition
3. A proof, process or product-detail section
4. One meaningful interaction tied to the business
5. A short, decisive CTA section

Do not finish after the hero. If the lower page has only repeated cards, tiny labels or empty whitespace, the build has failed the visual review.

## Functional requirements

Header links must reach real sections. Compact menus must open, close after navigation, close with Escape and restore focus. Every interactive control must have a real event handler and accessible state.

If the page includes a selector, accordion or tabs, pointer and keyboard input must work and exactly one matching panel must be visible where appropriate.

Forms must either save to a real Base44 entity or be omitted. Never display a fake success state for a form that does not submit.

## QA checklist

For the published route, check:

1. Native frontend route returns successfully
2. All media URLs return the expected media
3. Each chapter advances at separated scroll positions
4. Scroll reversal works
5. Each chapter lands cleanly before the next
6. Posters and still fallbacks work
7. Phone layout is intentionally composed
8. Reduced-motion mode removes long pinned travel
9. Data-saving mode avoids large media where detectable
10. Menu and navigation work
11. Business interaction works
12. Main CTA reaches its real action
13. No horizontal overflow or clipped type
14. Console errors are checked where the browser allows
15. No invented factual claims, fake forms, placeholder links or visible development instructions
16. Long headings fit at 390 pixels without clipping
17. The first visual chapter, second visual chapter and CTA each look intentionally different
18. The lower page still looks designed when the video is disabled

If a check fails, make one focused repair and rerun that check. If the shipped bundle does not change, report “No-op confirmed” with the old and new fingerprints. Do not loop through blind redesigns.

## Required report

Return:

- Chosen concept
- Page grammar
- Chapter list
- Actual route
- Media used and sizes
- Generation costs or unknown costs
- Files changed
- Checks passed
- Checks unverified
- Remaining limitations

Do not call the site a ten-thousand-dollar client package until the student has viewed it and approved the result.

name: premium-website-builder
description: Build complete cinematic business websites in a Base44 native frontend, with art-directed media, designed lower sections and verified scroll interactions. Use for premium website work, separate from the standard local-business and four-brand onboarding skills.
---

# Complete premium website builder

Build the entire website. The hero, reading sections, imagery, interactions and closing must belong to one business and one design direction. Do not stop at an impressive scroll video above generic sections.

## Evidence boundary

Version 3.0.0. Blake approved the five local website designs on 10 September 2026 and selected this as the premium website skill.

This method comes from five local Chromium-tested concepts: surf, restaurant, landscape, architecture and product. Both scroll films on all five were tested forward and backward, including decoded-frame checks. Mobile-width, keyboard, download, reduced-motion, data-saving and failed-media checks also ran locally. This skill has not yet passed a fresh Base44 Superagent run. Do not represent that unperformed test as complete.

The reference runtime and visual examples are in this package. The detailed research and test record are in `references/research-and-findings.md` and `references/local-test-results.md`; consult them for evidence and limitations. Read [references/design-and-media.md](references/design-and-media.md) when choosing the direction, and [references/base44-and-qa.md](references/base44-and-qa.md) when building and testing. Use [assets/runtime.js](assets/runtime.js) for the tested vanilla runtime mechanics. Adapt lifecycle and element ownership to the actual app framework rather than running a global DOM script blindly inside React.

## Start from a small brief

Use the business type, desired visitor action and any supplied assets. Infer a creative direction if the user delegated it. Ask one short question at a time only for material missing information, such as which real booking destination to use or whether imagery shows the client's actual products. Do not interview them about every font and section.

For a real business, separate supplied facts from missing facts. Use verified service information and client assets. Do not fabricate reviews, addresses, staff, prices, product specifications or commercial capabilities. For a concept, build meaningful discovery or a saved brief without pretending a booking, payment or enquiry was delivered. Follow the user's requested disclosure treatment; do not automatically append a large fictional-demo warning to every page.

Write in plain language, with no em dashes in authored copy. No decorative captions naming the effect, such as "The pour", "The pause", "The reveal" or "The scroll". Ordinary business copy may remain over footage when useful and readable.

## Plan the whole page before media

Save a concise, visible direction with these items:

- The business, visitor and real primary action.
- A specific visual idea, material palette, two type roles and image treatment.
- A section map from first screen to footer. Each section has a job, composition, copy requirement and media asset.
- A shot list: camera starting position, one achievable movement, ending composition, text-safe area and phone treatment.
- Known facts, required client content and proposed assets. Ask for missing content that materially affects the site instead of filling gaps with generic prose.

Research a few relevant real websites or creator case studies when needed. Inspect more than the hero. Extract concrete composition and content decisions. Do not copy another business's photography, claims or identity.

Use one or two film chapters when their stories justify them. For a two-film site, put a substantial editorial or product/service section between them. A longer video is not automatically a better or smoother experience. Match the motion to the business and the scroll travel to the actual clip.

## Generate a coherent asset set

Use the provider the user selected. For Envato, prepare copyable prompts and let the user perform the manual generation handoff. For an authorised Kie route, use its current documented schema and a saved secret on the agent. Never expose that secret in frontend code, chat, uploaded files or a reference package.

State the known cost or allowance before spending. Respect existing approval and the agreed bound. Persist the request and task ID; poll an existing task after an interruption. An ambiguous paid submission is not permission to generate again.

An initial media set often needs a hero, one meaningful detail image, one environment or use-context image, and an optional second film. Assign aspect ratios to the sections, not to a universal "landscape" rule. Portrait lower-section images were useful in the five-site test.

Inspect the starting images yourself before animating. Check shape, typography contamination, material, lighting, background and crop. For multiple views of one exact product, use the approved hero/client photograph as a reference. A common text prompt alone did not preserve the lamp design in the local test.

Prompt each film as one manageable action with stable geometry. An architectural glide, reflected light, fire or water movement is easier to evaluate than a long chain of unrelated actions. Do not promise that any generator will reliably execute an arbitrary fly-through.

Inspect distributed frames including the ending. Trim a weak tail when that preserves the intended story. Ask for a reroll only when the actual defect warrants its cost. Keep originals outside published assets, process web versions and record source/processed sizes and actual costs.

## Build the native frontend

Discover the current app tools and framework. Preserve the intended app identity and existing unrelated pages. Use Base44 app pages and its normal frontend pipeline, with scoped styles and actual handlers. Do not serve interactive HTML through backend functions or rely on CSS injected by the chat preview.

Use the selected reference's proportions as a starting point, not a universal template. Make the lower half strong: meaningful images, varied compositions, readable type and specific content. A bigger section number or more padding is not a design pass.

For scroll media:

- Keep the poster, headline and primary action useful before media loads.
- On eligible screens, use native page scroll with sticky media. Never intercept the wheel.
- Calculate progress from section height minus sticky-element height. Clamp target time to duration minus a small end tolerance.
- Schedule updates through requestAnimationFrame; serialise seeks and apply the newest target after completion. Bound repeated stalled seeks and fall back cleanly.
- Load the second clip near its section, not at initial page load. Stop unnecessary work offscreen or when the tab is hidden.
- A complete Blob fetch enables local seeking after download. It requires successful fetch/CORS, not HTTP Range. Do not claim progressive playback while building that Blob.
- If using direct loading instead, test actual-host seeking rather than assuming it works.
- Clean up observers, listeners, timers, requests and Blob URLs on unmount. Restore behaviour on history navigation.

At phone widths, reduced motion and detectable data saving, choose the still mode before fetching video. Remove pinned travel. A failed request switches to usable still mode and stops further retries, but it cannot undo bytes already downloaded. Compose the phone subject and type intentionally; do not copy a universal 65% crop.

Use actual section links and keyboard-accessible controls. Menus open, close after navigation, close with Escape and return focus. Selected choices expose state and exactly one panel. Connect client actions to their actual destination and test the result. Never simulate a successful transaction.

## Verify and deliver

Follow the browser checks in [references/base44-and-qa.md](references/base44-and-qa.md). Review full-page screenshots after ensuring lazy images have loaded. Inspect the post-hero sections individually. Test at desktop, around 780px and 375px. Fix observed defects, then rerun the affected checks.

Distinguish editor build state from public deployment. An unchanged public fingerprint does not establish that no code was written. Verify the exact route and target app, inspect the builder response or source when available, then determine whether publication is pending. If only the owner can publish, ask once after the work is concrete. Do not send repeated build requests to solve an unverified publish gap.

Return actual URLs, a concise visual comparison if there are variants, checks passed, checks unverified, exact changed files and visible generation/Base44 costs. A green status, HTTP 200, source-code string or screenshot alone is not proof of functioning motion.

Do not call the result client-ready or repeatedly reproducible solely because one fictional local build passes. The user judges aesthetic quality; real integrations, physical mobile behaviour and a fresh-agent Base44 test have their own evidence requirements.

# Base44 implementation and evidence

## Build and publish are separate states

Record the app ID, app name, exact editor route, exact public route and current public fingerprint. The root can show a diagnostic page while the intended page exists at another route. Do not diagnose a failed build by looking only at `/`.

After sending a builder instruction, retain the response, task ID and claimed files when the tools expose them. If the public bundle is unchanged, possible explanations include unpublished changes, the wrong route/app, a failed build or stale deployment. That evidence alone cannot distinguish them.

Inspect the editor/source/preview when supported. If changes are present there but not public, mark publication pending. If publishing is not available to the Superagent, ask the owner for the single publish action after the code is ready to review. Do not wait indefinitely or resend the same build to fix publication.

Use the supported Base44 native frontend. Do not deploy interactive HTML as a backend function. Do not infer tool schemas or app-creation/publishing authority from another agent's transcript.

## Runtime adaptation

`../assets/runtime.js` is a tested local vanilla reference, not a React component. When adapting:

- Use refs for the film section, sticky element and video.
- Create one controller per mounted film and clean it up from the effect.
- Bind menu and selector state through the framework's normal handlers.
- Keep frame updates out of React state.
- Keep one source of truth for selected panels and ARIA state.
- Do not let shared styles leak into unrelated Base44 pages.
- Load stored assets from stable final URLs. Recheck CORS if fetch crosses origins.

Choose the film/still mode before setting `src`. Phones, reduced motion and data saving should make zero video requests. A failed video load switches to still mode and removes long travel. It necessarily starts with a failed attempt, so report failure bytes honestly.

## Minimum actual-browser checks

Run each on the exact frontend route and record passed, failed or unverified:

1. Native page renders with final stored images and the intended content.
2. Both film sections load on an eligible desktop viewport.
3. Scroll to separated points near 0%, 25%, 60% and the ending, then reverse to 30%. Record actual currentTime and expected duration mapping. Observe decoded frames or screenshots as well as the number.
4. Scroll normally and rapidly. Seeking catches up without freezing or trapping the page. The clip is paused while time is controlled by scroll.
5. The first screen is useful before loading. The main button and skip link reach the promised sections.
6. All navigation and back-to-top links reach real targets.
7. Compact menu opens, closes after navigation and with Escape, and returns focus to its trigger.
8. Keyboard and pointer activate every choice; exactly one corresponding panel appears with correct selected state.
9. A real form/booking/checkout integration produces its actual intended outcome. For a concept using a download, verify the generated file contains the selected information. Do not substitute a success toast for delivery.
10. At about 780px and 375px, inspect whole-page layout, overflow, clipped headings, image crops and contrast. Hiding overflow is not a repair for oversized content.
11. Reduced motion and detectable data saving use still layout with zero video requests.
12. Deliberately abort a video request. The page remains usable without pinned empty space. Restore normal assets after the test.
13. Inspect browser console and page errors. Source grep alone is not console verification.
14. Navigate away and back. The media controller works again.
15. Search authored copy for em dashes, placeholder links, invented facts, fake claims and development instructions.

For full-page screenshots, let lazy images actually load first. A screenshot tool can otherwise capture empty offscreen images even though they load during ordinary scrolling. State when mobile results are emulation rather than a physical phone.

## Evidence and stopping

Save concise raw measurements, screenshots, generation task IDs, original/processed media sizes, final URLs, changed files and public fingerprint. Record Base44 credit use only if visible. Do not guess it from elapsed time.

Fix a demonstrated issue and rerun the affected checks. A repeated unresolved blocker should be reported with the precise failing operation. Do not declare the builder broken from a public fingerprint alone and do not spend repeated calls on that assumption.

The local five-site test establishes technical behaviour of its reference implementation. A fresh Base44 agent must still port and test it. User aesthetic approval, client content and real business integrations are separate release conditions.

# Whole-page design and media decisions

Read this when creating the direction and shot list. The five examples are tested references, not a rule to reuse the same content on unrelated businesses.

| Business | Governing idea | Lower-page composition | Primary and second motion |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Surf | Athletic, salty, direct | Oversized condensed statement; offset portrait surfboards; contrasting session chooser | Existing surfer ride; separate empty-barrel water shot |
| Restaurant | Unhurried, warm, tactile | Centred serif editorial opening; small seal; portrait food beside ruled menu | Slow kitchen dolly; close fire and grill detail |
| Landscape | Organic form with structural clarity | Open introduction; two-column prose; tall botanical crop and material swatches | Travel along curved path; water bowl and grasses |
| Architecture | Space, margin and material | Separate masthead; wide project study; narrower staircase photograph and text | Exterior parallax; interior move toward courtyard |
| Product | Quiet object, close attention | Precise typography and material rows; full product-in-room image | Stable lamp with camera parallax; metal reflection study |

The source-only HTML/CSS examples in `../assets/source-layouts/` show exact compositions that were rendered locally. Their images and fonts live in the separate complete reference-site archive; these source copies are not standalone runnable pages. View the approved full-page compositions in `screenshots/surf.jpg`, `screenshots/restaurant.jpg`, `screenshots/landscape.jpg`, `screenshots/architecture.jpg` and `screenshots/product.jpg`. They are starting references. Keep their relationship between media, content and spacing rather than copying a section name or enforcing a hard-coded height on every business.

## The section brief

For every section write: visitor question, content answering it, composition, asset, action and phone arrangement. Example:

- Visitor question: what kind of food is this?
- Content: three approved dishes, their components and a route to the full menu or reservation.
- Composition: a portrait food image beside a narrow ruled menu, with a large serif introduction.
- Asset: one sharp overhead plated dish, burgundy linen, warm ceramics, matching the hero's palette.
- Action: the real booking or menu destination. In the local lab, save an evening direction instead of a fake booking.
- Phone: image first, menu below, readable rules and generous tap areas.

If the business has no real menu, project images, service information or actual booking destination, do not fill the gaps with invented commercial claims. Ask for the missing high-value content. The agent should be proactive about requesting useful assets, not passive about accepting an empty page.

## Shot briefing

Keep a shared art-direction paragraph: material, light direction, palette, contrast, lens character and realism. Add a separate composition instruction for each intended crop. Keep text as HTML.

For stills, specify the complete subject, its position, the scene around it and exclusions that matter. For an actual client product, use a reference image. Generated identity must be visually compared across views. The local lamp's first room view changed its stem; a reference edit corrected it.

For motion, specify one shot, camera path, subject movement, invariant geometry and a clear ending. "Cinematic, premium, smooth" alone leaves too much unspecified. A request to orbit a product, fly through a window, follow a person and change scenes in one clip has multiple independent failure points.

The local Kie pilot used Seedream 4.5 basic and Kling 2.5 Turbo image-to-video Pro. These names and prices are observations, not permanent defaults. Verify current model support and account cost before a new job. The tested Kling route accepted `image_url`, `prompt`, `duration`, `negative_prompt` and `cfg_scale`. Do not add unsupported resolution or quality fields and then claim they were applied.

Use the provided source prompts in `../assets/media-prompts.json` only when their composition is relevant. They record the real jobs used by the lab. Do not regenerate reference media already attached to an import task.

## Quality gate

Inspect at least six points across a clip, including its ending. Check geometry, continuity, composition and usable detail. Inspect the actual phone crop. Reject legible nonsense branding and inconsistent client products. Trim a weak tail if the retained action still works. The surf source was trimmed before its weak ending; the shorter result is preferable to a flawed longer clip.

A ten-second clip can make a longer progression possible, but duration alone does not improve seeking. Encode and test it. The reference processing used H.264/yuv420p, no audio, faststart, GOP 8 and CRF 23 at 1600px width, preserving aspect ratio. Those settings produced roughly 1.4 to 7.4 MB clips in this batch. Tune and inspect rather than promising a fixed file size or load time.

Avoid placeholder intensity. Large numerals, extra empty padding and giant slogans do not replace supporting imagery or content. Judge the whole page at once, then inspect the important sections at readable scale.

# Five-site premium website test

10 September 2026. Blake reviewed the local collection and approved its appearance, then explicitly requested this method become the premium website skill.

## Actual builds

Open http://localhost:8766/index.html while the local server is running. The websites are served from `site/` and remain separate from existing Base44 apps.

## What ran

- Chromium through Python Playwright, real localhost pages.
- Ten film sections: currentTime sampled near 0%, 25%, 60%, 95%, then reversed to 30%, with tolerance 0.25 seconds.
- Independent decoded-frame pass at 0%, 50%, 100%, then 25%. Every film produced different decoded frames. Videos remain paused while scrolling controls time.
- Menu Escape, focus restoration and close-after-navigation at 375px.
- All three selector options activated by keyboard; exactly one selected state and panel. Actual text downloads saved to evidence/.
- Real anchor targets, source asset loading and console/pageerror collection. Zero normal-run console or runtime errors recorded.
- Width and text-overflow measurements at 780px and 375px. No measured overflow or clipped text in the checked headings, paragraphs and options.
- Phone, reduced-motion and data-saving sessions: zero MP4 requests. Forced video failures: usable still sections with pinned travel removed.
- Browser navigation away and back restores scroll mode.
- Full-page desktop and phone screenshots plus per-film timeline screenshots inspected.

## Repairs and rejected material

The old generated watch was rejected for invented lettering. The new product concept uses an unbranded lamp. Its room view initially had a different stem, so one reference edit corrected that still. The existing surfer footage was trimmed to 6.8 seconds before its weak tail. The surf and lamp phone crops were repaired with separate image and headline regions. Header contrast was strengthened. Browser-history restoration was added to the media lifecycle. Offscreen lazy images were explicitly loaded before full-page evidence screenshots, avoiding misleading empty image regions in the capture.

Final new batch: **624 Kie credits**. Starting balance 702.3, ending balance 78.3. Provider-reported per-job deductions reconcile with that difference. Twelve image jobs including the reference edit cost 78 credits; nine video jobs cost 546 credits. The budget bound was 650 credits. No duplicate jobs or video rerolls were purchased. Earlier stills and the existing surf clip are reused assets with separate previous costs.

Originals are outside the served `site/` directory. Kie key access is outside the frontend. Final web clips use local assets, not temporary provider URLs. All fonts are served locally. The following sizes are from actual processed files:

## Limits

These are local business concepts with real navigation, selection and downloadable briefs. They do not process real bookings, payments or enquiries. Those integrations need actual client destinations and testing. The architecture/project imagery and product studies are generated concepts, not claims of built client work or saleable product specifications.

Mobile results are Chromium viewport emulation, not a physical iPhone or Android device. Live network performance, Safari and real Base44 app execution remain unverified. No Base44 credits were spent or measured in this local run. Active build time was not instrumented precisely and is unknown; provider generation durations remain in the raw job records. Do not infer them from chat gaps.

User aesthetic approval is recorded, but no price/value guarantee follows. A fresh-agent Base44 import and later small-brief generation test remain separate course validations.


# Premium websites: research translated into five working tests

Research and implementation date: 10 September 2026.

## Finding

The reliable starting point is a complete visual and content system, followed by media generation and implementation. A scroll video is one component. It does not solve content hierarchy, image consistency, section composition, navigation or the visitor's next action.

This experiment builds five different business concepts with the same small motion runtime, but different typography, image crops, section arrangements, colours and content. Each has a moving hero, ordinary editorial sections, a second moving chapter and a working direction selector with a text download. This deliberately tests a two-film pattern requested by Blake. It is not a prescription that every client site should have two pinned films.

The evidence supports local technical feasibility. It does not establish a sale price, conversion uplift, fresh-agent repeatability, Base44 deployment success or Blake's aesthetic approval.

## What the references actually contribute

The source selection combines creator-written case studies with real business websites. These are useful exemplars, not an objective ranking of the world's best websites. The live business sites were inspected through their public content; their entire interactive experience was not audited here.

| Source | Observation supported by the source | Decision tested here |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PX PUSH, creator case study [1] | One governing identity connects its effects and components. Its creators discuss shared animation lifecycle and native mobile scrolling. | Give every business a coherent material and type system; share lifecycle mechanics rather than a universal visual template. |
| Troa portfolio, creator case study [2] | The project discusses rendering, media and performance decisions as part of the experience. | Process originals into smaller web files, retain stills and load the second film near its section. |
| NOD bootcamp, creator case study [3] | Pacing and motion were considered in prototypes, with a distinction between triggered and scroll-controlled movement. | Separate the pinned chapters with real reading space. Do not attach scrubbing to every paragraph. |
| Norm Architects [4] | The portfolio organises work into architectural and design categories, with named projects and substantive descriptions. | The architecture test needs a project composition and material context after its hero, rather than another generic benefit grid. |
| Audo Copenhagen [5] | Product discovery is supported by furniture, lighting, accessories and contextual brand content. | A product needs to be seen in use and in detail, not only as a single studio cutout. |
| Noma [6] | The restaurant site provides routes into its dining and wider food world. | The restaurant test connects atmosphere with food and a meaningful planning action. A cinematic kitchen cannot replace menu content. |
| Aesop [7] | The site combines product discovery, explanatory content and shopping actions. | Visual restraint still needs concrete content and a usable destination. Spaciousness is not a substitute for information. |

These are design inferences from the sources, not claims that adopting a font or effect reproduces their commercial results. No source code, photography or brand assets from these reference businesses were copied into the five concepts.

## Attributes that matter below the hero

1. **Image coverage.** Plan a subject, an environment, a detail and an in-context view. Assign each an actual section and crop before paying for generation. We generated portrait food, planting, surfboard, architectural and room images specifically for lower sections.
2. **Continuity.** Lighting, material, lens character and colour should belong to the same world. Prompting separate images with the same vague style did not fully preserve the lamp's shape. A reference-guided correction was needed for the room image. Exact client products should use approved reference photography and comparisons, not imagined specifications.
3. **Hierarchy at section scale.** One dominant headline or photograph per composition, enough body copy to explain its purpose, and a real onward action. A large numeral and excess padding do not supply substance.
4. **Different rhythms.** Surf uses condensed type, an offset board photograph and acid-colour selection section. Restaurant uses centred serif composition, a seal, food photograph and ruled menu. Landscape uses botanical form and material swatches. Architecture separates the masthead from the film and uses differently sized project images. Product uses a studio view, material details and an interior scene.
5. **Editorial variety without random decoration.** Across a page, vary image size, column balance, background and information density. Across businesses, change the visual grammar, not just the accent colour.
6. **A specific visitor task.** The local prototypes allow a visitor to choose and download a relevant brief. They do not pretend to book a table, sell a lamp or send an enquiry to fictional staff. A real client version needs the actual booking, enquiry or checkout integration and approved business information.
7. **Mobile art direction.** A universal 65% crop failed for the surfer and lamp. Both were repaired using an image-led upper region and ordinary headline area below. Other compositions retained full-height photography. The subject and readable type both matter.
8. **Motion pacing.** A 10-second source does not require ten seconds of scrolling. The section's physical travel determines how quickly the visitor samples it. Here the hero starts at 250vh and the second chapter at 185vh; these are tested starting values, not a quality metric.
9. **Restraint between peaks.** The middle sections remain usable without animation. Extra particles, decorative caption beats, cursor replacements and perpetual loops would compete with the film and the content.
10. **Evidence on the actual build.** Time samples, decoded frames, browser interactions and screenshots answer different questions. None can replace the others.

## Generation experiment

Provider: Kie. Stills: Seedream 4.5 basic, including a later reference-guided edit. New videos: Kling 2.5 Turbo image-to-video Pro. Four 10-second heroes and five 5-second detail chapters. The existing surf hero was reused and trimmed to 6.8 seconds before its weak ending.

The nine new video jobs all completed. Each was inspected in six distributed frames. Their deliberately restrained motions preserved the principal environment or object well enough for these concepts. This does not demonstrate reliable complex choreography, exact surfing physics, continuous multi-location fly-throughs or exact product reproduction.

The watched outputs show clear motion in the environment films. The lamp and ember films are subtler. Longer is not automatically more dramatic: motion amplitude and camera path need to be explicit in the shot brief. A future high-energy concept should test a carefully bounded action independently before committing the whole page to it.

The new batch initially used 617.5 credits: 71.5 for eleven text-to-image stills and 546 for nine videos. A single image correction was then added within the disclosed 650-credit cap. The final cost and each provider-reported deduction are recorded in media-state.json and TEST-REPORT.md. Previous completed stills and the existing surf video are reused assets with earlier costs, not included in this batch subtotal. Current published pricing is a snapshot, not a promise for future students. [8][9]

No authenticated API key is shipped in the site. Generation records remain outside the served site directory. A job is persisted before its paid submission and is polled by task ID. An ambiguous response must be investigated rather than resubmitted automatically.

## Implementation experiment

The local pages use explicit CSS and vanilla JavaScript. This avoids a hidden dependency on a chat preview's injected Tailwind stylesheet. The shared film runtime reads actual sticky travel, updates on scroll using requestAnimationFrame, serialises seeks and follows the latest target after seek completion. Repeated seek stalls trigger still mode. It stops scheduling offscreen and when the document is hidden, aborts pending fetches on teardown, revokes Blob URLs, and restores the runtime when returning from browser history.

Desktop media is fetched into a complete local Blob. That enables seeking from the downloaded file without requiring the simple local HTTP server to provide range requests. It is not progressive playback. The poster remains visible while fetching. The second clip waits until its section approaches the viewport. A real Base44 deployment must test CORS, storage URLs and seeking again.

At widths of 700px and below, reduced motion or detectable data saving, the runtime never assigns a video source or fetches a clip. Failure mode necessarily attempts the initial request before discovering failure; it then collapses the pinned travel and retains useful content. It cannot truthfully promise zero downloaded bytes for a failure that occurs after a request begins.

H.264, yuv420p, no audio, short GOP and faststart were used. The processed videos range from roughly 1.4 MB to 7.4 MB. Originals are retained outside the public assets. All ten clips have matching posters. Source and processed sizes are in processing.json.

## What the five tests can and cannot establish

The browser harness tests both videos at separated positions and reverses the scroll. A second pass samples decoded video frames, so a changing currentTime property alone is insufficient. It exercises all three options with the keyboard, downloads the actual selected brief, checks anchors, examines page and console errors, and inspects widths of 780 and 375 pixels. It also aborts video requests and separately tests reduced motion and data saving.

Those checks concern a local Chromium browser, including phone-sized emulation. They do not establish physical iPhone Safari behaviour, actual mobile-network performance, live client transactions or Base44 frontend compatibility. These limits are explicit in the result record.

The old premium-lab-v1 result should not be used as course evidence. Its five variants were not a reliable five-business motion test. This v2 folder is an isolated replacement experiment, with raw evidence and a reproducible harness.

## Course consequence

Keep the standard local-business workflow separate. Make cinematic websites an advanced path where the agent takes responsibility for the whole page and asks for missing client content only where it matters. Do not inflate message count for its own sake. Useful stages are a complete visual/content direction, approved media, working frontend, and evidence-based refinement.

The next Base44 test should port one approved local reference using its actual assets, then verify its native app route. Record editor state and published state separately. An unchanged public bundle proves only that the public deployment did not change; it does not prove the builder wrote no source. The earlier failure loop came from collapsing those states.

A fresh-agent test using the finished skill is still required before describing the course workflow as repeatable. One successful local implementation by its author is not that experiment.

<!-- DRAFT v1 for Blake's review. Blake is writing his own version of this skill; his draft supersedes this one. Written 2026-08-26 from his archetype taxonomy. -->
---
name: website-archetypes
description: How to build the right website for any local business with no website. Five archetypes, what each one must showcase, what public sources to pull from, and hard rules that keep every site truthful. Used whenever we build a demo or client site.
---

# Website Archetypes

Every local business fits one of five archetypes. Pick the archetype first. It decides what the website must showcase, what order the sections go in, and what tone the words carry. Then build from the Client Brief and public sources only.

## How to choose
Ask: what makes a customer pick THIS kind of business? That answer is the archetype.

## Archetype A: SHOW YOUR WORK
- Who: trades. Plumbers, electricians, builders, roofers, landscapers, painters, tilers.
- The customer's question: "can I trust these people with my house?"
- Must showcase: real jobs done, reviews with names, years in the game, the areas they cover, one big Call Now button that follows you down the page.
- Section order: name + what they do + call button, services, reviews, recent work photos, area + hours, get a quote form.
- Tone words: solid, straight, local, no fluff. Short sentences. The site should feel like a firm handshake.
- Public sources to pull: Google Business Profile (services, hours, phone, reviews word for word), their Facebook page for job photos, review text for the exact phrases customers use.

## Archetype B: SHOW YOUR SPACE
- Who: hospitality and retail. Cafes, restaurants, bars, salons, barbers, boutiques, gyms.
- The customer's question: "do I want to be in there?"
- Must showcase: the space and the vibe, photos first and biggest, the menu or service list, opening hours, how to find them, booking or walk-in.
- Section order: big photo + name + one inviting line, photos of the space and the product, menu or services, reviews, hours + map + address, book or visit.
- Tone words: warm, inviting, a little fun. The site should feel like being welcomed in.
- Public sources: Google photos and reviews, their Instagram and Facebook for the good photos, posted menus, review quotes about atmosphere.

## Archetype C: SHOW YOUR EXPERTISE
- Who: professionals. Lawyers, accountants, consultants, brokers, financial advisers, physios.
- The customer's question: "do these people know what they're doing?"
- Must showcase: what they're qualified in, the exact services, who they help, plain-English answers to common questions, a professional but human tone, an easy first step (call or enquiry).
- Section order: name + specialty + one credibility line, services in plain English, who we help, common questions answered simply, reviews, contact.
- Tone words: calm, clear, credible. No jargon. The site should feel like good advice.
- Public sources: Google profile, their LinkedIn if public, review quotes about outcomes and trust, listed credentials. Never state a qualification that is not published somewhere.

## Archetype D: SHOW RESULTS
- Who: mobile services. Cleaners, pest control, mobile mechanics, detailers, lawn care, pressure washing.
- The customer's question: "what will it look like when they're done?"
- Must showcase: before and after energy, what's included in each service, speed and convenience (we come to you), reviews, easy booking or quote.
- Section order: name + the result in one line + call button, services with what's included, results photos (or clean placeholders), reviews, areas covered, quote form.
- Tone words: quick, reliable, done properly. The site should feel like a job crossed off a list.
- Public sources: Google profile and reviews, Facebook before/after photos, review quotes about reliability and results.

## Archetype E: SHOW YOUR INVENTORY
- Who: businesses whose product IS a list. Real estate agencies, car yards, equipment and plant hire, furniture stores.
- The customer's question: "what have you got right now?"
- Must showcase: current stock or listings front and centre, clean cards with photo + key facts + price where public, how to enquire on a specific item, the business's credibility second.
- Section order: name + what they stock + one line, the inventory grid, how buying or hiring works, reviews, contact.
- Tone words: organised, current, easy to browse. The site should feel like a well-lit showroom.
- Public sources: their public listings pages, Google profile, only items that are verifiably current, marked "example stock" if currency is unknown.

## Hard rules, every archetype, no exceptions
1. Never invent facts, services, prices, qualifications, or history. Everything on the site comes from the Client Brief or a public source you can name.
2. Reviews are quoted word for word with the reviewer's first name. Never reworded, never invented, never trimmed to change meaning.
3. Real phone number, real hours, real address. One wrong digit makes the student look like a scammer.
4. Missing photos get clean generic placeholders, and you tell the student exactly which images are placeholders so they can ask the owner for real ones.
5. Anything missing from the brief gets a clean placeholder and a note to the student, never a guess.
6. Mobile first. Every site must read well and dial the phone from a phone.
7. One page unless the student asks for more. A great one-pager beats a thin five-pager.
Pick a skill on the left.
